Appendix A
Foundations of Dual Dynamic Smoothing and One-Step Progress

A fundamental difficulty in minimax optimization is the potential nonsmoothness of the primal objective function

f(x):=\max_{y\in{\cal Y}}{\cal L}(x,y),

which can occur even when the payoff
{\cal L}
is smooth. To address this challenge, we employ a quadratic regularization approach to approximate
f
with a smooth surrogate. This smoothing technique was pioneered in standard optimization by
Nesterov (2005)
and
Beck and Teboulle (2012)
, and was recently extended to projection-free minimax settings by
Boroun et al. (2023)
.
In this section, we first introduce the formal
dual-smoothing framework
and establish its key regularity properties. We then analyze the
one-step progress
of the iterates under various technical conditions, considering update rules based on both LMO and PO for the primal and dual variables. This analysis provides the necessary recursions to establish the convergence rates of our single-loop algorithms.

A.1
The Dual Smoothing Framework

Given a payoff function
{\cal L}:{\cal X}\times{\cal Y}\to{\mathbb{R}}
, a reference point
y_{0}\in{\cal Y}
, and a regularization parameter
\beta\geq 0
, we define the regularized payoff function
{\cal L}_{\beta}
and the resulting regularized primal function
f_{\beta}
as follows

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y)

\displaystyle:={\cal L}(x,y)-\frac{\beta}{2}\|y-y_{0}\|^{2}

(
12
a)

\displaystyle f_{\beta}(x)

\displaystyle:=\max_{y\in{\cal Y}}{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y).

(
12
b)

For any
\beta>0
, the function
{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,\cdot)
is strongly concave in
y
, ensuring that for any
x\in{\cal X}
, there exists a unique maximizer

\displaystyle y_{\beta}^{\star}(x):=\argmax_{y\in{\cal Y}}{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y).

(
12
c)

The following lemma establishes the key regularity properties of this construction, providing the analytical foundation for our smoothing framework.

Lemma A.1
.

Suppose
Assumption

1.1
holds. For any
x,x^{\prime}\in{\cal X}
,
y\in{\cal Y}
and
\beta\geq\beta^{\prime}\geq 0
with
\max\{\beta,\mu\}>0
, we have

(i)

\frac{\beta+\mu}{2}\|y_{\beta}^{\star}(x)-y\|^{2}\leq{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y_{\beta}^{\star}(x))-{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y)
.

(ii)

{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y_{\beta}^{\star}(x))-{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y)\leq\frac{2}{\beta+\mu}\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y)\|^{2}.

(iii)

\|y_{\beta}^{\star}(x)-y_{\beta}^{\star}(x^{\prime})\|\leq\frac{L_{yx}}{\beta+\mu}\|x-x^{\prime}\|.

(iv)

f_{\beta}
is differentiable with
\nabla f_{\beta}(x)=\nabla_{x}{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y_{\beta}^{\star}(x))=\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y_{\beta}^{\star}(x))
on an open set containing
{\cal X}
.

(v)

\nabla f_{\beta}
is Lipschitz continuous over
X
with the corresponding constant
L_{f_{\beta}}:=L_{xx}+{L_{yx}^{2}}/{(\beta+\mu)}
.

(vi)

0\leq f_{\beta^{\prime}}(x)-f_{\beta}(x)\leq\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}(\beta-\beta^{\prime})}{2}.

Proof.

Assertion
(i)
is a direct consequence of the
(\beta+\mu)
-strong concavity of
{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,\cdot)
with respect to
y
, which follows immediately from its definition. To prove assertion
(ii)
, we use the concavity of
{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,\cdot)
. For any
y\in{\cal Y}
,

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y_{\beta}^{\star}(x))-{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y)^{\top}(y_{\beta}^{\star}(x)-y)

\displaystyle\leq\|y_{\beta}^{\star}(x)-y\|\cdot\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y)\|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{2}{\beta+\mu}\left({\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y_{\beta}^{\star}(x))-{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y)\right)}\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y)\|,

where the first inequality follows from the concavity of
{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,\cdot)
, the second inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, and the last inequality follows from
Lemma

A.1

(i)
. Squaring both sides of the resulting inequality yields the desired claim.
Assertion
(iii)
follows directly from the results established in
Lin et al. (2020a, Lemma 4.3)
. Assertion
(iv)
follows from Danskin’s theorem
(
Bertsekas, 1999
, Proposition B.25(a))
, which is applicable because the condition
\max\{\beta,\mu\}>0
guarantees a unique solution
y^{\star}_{\beta}(x)
to the maximization problem in (
12b
). Furthermore, Danskin’s theorem further implies that
\nabla_{x}{\cal L}_{\beta}(x,y^{\star}_{\beta}(x))=\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y^{\star}_{\beta}(x))
.
Assertion
(v)
follows directly from the analysis provided in
Lin et al. (2020a, Lemma 4.3)
.
Finally, to prove assertion
(vi)
, the condition
\beta\geq\beta^{\prime}
directly implies that for any
x\in{\cal X}
and
y\in{\cal Y}
, we have

{\cal L}(x,y)-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2}\|y-y_{0}\|^{2}\geq{\cal L}(x,y)-\frac{\beta}{2}\|y-y_{0}\|^{2}.

By maximizing both sides over
y\in{\cal Y}
and using the definition of the regularized primal function, we obtain
f_{\beta}(x)\leq f_{\beta^{\prime}}(x)
, which establishes the lower bound. For the upper bound, note that

{\cal L}(x,y)-\frac{\beta^{\prime}}{2}\|y-y_{0}\|^{2}={\cal L}(x,y)-\frac{\beta}{2}\|y-y_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{\beta-\beta^{\prime}}{2}\|y-y_{0}\|^{2}\leq{\cal L}(x,y)-\frac{\beta}{2}\|y-y_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}(\beta-\beta^{\prime})}{2}.

Maximizing both sides over
y\in{\cal Y}
and using the definition of the regularized primal function yield the desired upper bound. This completes the proof.
∎

Since we dynamically update the smoothing parameter
\beta
at each iteration
t
, we define the following shorthands to simplify our subsequent analysis

{\cal L}_{t}(x,y):={\cal L}_{\beta_{t}}(x,y),\quad f_{t}(x):=f_{\beta_{t}}(x),\quad y_{t}^{\star}(x):=y_{\beta_{t}}^{\star}(x).

(
13
)

A.2
One-Step Progress for Single-Loop Algorithms

Standard gradient methods applied to
f_{\beta}
require the
exact
maximizer
y_{\beta}^{\star}(x)
to compute
\nabla f_{\beta}(x)
. However, this requires solving an inner optimization problem at every iteration, resulting in a computationally expensive double-loop structure. Furthermore, the smoothing parameter
\beta
presents additional challenges. A fixed choice of
\beta
introduces a persistent approximation error, whereas dynamically decreasing
\beta
toward zero causes the smoothness constant of
f_{\beta}
to explode. To achieve a more efficient single-loop architecture, our framework circumvents the inner maximization entirely. We instead perform a single oracle query, using either a linear minimization oracle or a projection oracle, directly on the regularized payoff function
{\cal L}_{\beta}
. To characterize the progress of these updates, we define a discrepancy term
H_{t}
that approximately quantifies the distance of the current dual iterate from the regularized optimum. Using the shorthand (
13
), we define the discrepancy at iteration
t
as follows

\displaystyle H_{t}:={\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\geq 0,\quad\forall t\geq 0.

(
14
)

Thanks to
Lemma

A.1

(i)
, we have

\frac{\beta_{t}+\mu}{2}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|^{2}\leq H_{t}.

This relationship shoes that
H_{t}
provides a conservative estimate of the squared distance between the dual iterate and the unique maximizer of the regularized payoff.

The following lemma establishes a recursive bound between linking
H_{t}
with the regularized payoff and primal functions.

Lemma A.2
.

Suppose Assumption
1.1
holds. Let
\{x_{t},y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be any sequence in
{\cal X}\times{\cal Y}
, and let
\{\beta_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a non-increasing sequence of smoothing parameters such that
\max\{\beta_{t},\mu\}>0
. Then, for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\geq H_{t+1}-\frac{\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)}{\sqrt{\beta_{t+1}+\mu}}\sqrt{H_{t+1}}-2L_{xx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}.

Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t+1}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}+L_{yx}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}+L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2},

where the smoothness constant is defined as
L_{f_{t}}:=L_{xx}+L_{yx}^{2}/(\beta_{t}+\mu)
.

Proof.

We first prove the first inequality. Using the definition of
y_{t}^{\star}
and the Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla_{x}{\cal L}
, we expand the payoff function at iteration
t
around
x_{t+1}
. For the regularized optimal response, we have

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))

\displaystyle\geq{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))

\displaystyle\geq{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))^{\top}(x_{t}-x_{t+1})-\frac{L_{xx}}{2}\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle={\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))^{\top}(x_{t}-x_{t+1})-\frac{L_{xx}}{2}\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|^{2},

Similarly, for the dual iterate at
t+1
, we have

\displaystyle-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\geq-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})^{\top}(x_{t}-x_{t+1})-\frac{L_{xx}}{2}\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})^{\top}(x_{t}-x_{t+1})-\frac{L_{xx}}{2}\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|^{2}.

By summing these two inequalities, we obtain a lower bound for the difference

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\geq{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\quad+(\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{\mu}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1}))^{\top}(x_{t}-x_{t+1})-L_{xx}\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\quad-\|\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})\|\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|-L_{xx}\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\quad-(L_{xx}\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|+L_{yx}\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t+1}\|)\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|-L_{xx}\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})-L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|\cdot\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t+1}\|-2L_{xx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2},

where the third inequality follows from Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla_{x}{\cal L}
. Using the definition of
{\cal L}_{t}
, the difference term
{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})
in the last inequality can be written in terms of
H_{t+1}
as follows

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle={\cal L}_{t+1}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))-{\cal L}_{t+1}(x_{t+1},y_{t+1})+\frac{\beta_{t+1}-\beta_{t}}{2}\left(\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{0}\|^{2}-\|y_{t+1}-y_{0}\|^{2}\right)

\displaystyle=H_{t+1}+\frac{\beta_{t+1}-\beta_{t}}{2}\left(\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{0}\|^{2}-\|y_{t+1}-y_{0}\|^{2}\right).

By the property
\|a\|^{2}-\|b\|^{2}=(a-b)^{\top}(a+b)
and the boundedness of
{\cal Y}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{0}\|^{2}-\|y_{t+1}-y_{0}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=(y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t+1})^{\top}(y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{0}+y_{t+1}-y_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t+1}\|\left(\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{0}\|+\|y_{t+1}-y_{0}\|\right)

\displaystyle\leq 2D_{{\cal Y}}\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t+1}\|,

where the second inequality follows from the Cauchy-Schwartz and triangle inequality. Combining these results, we have

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\geq H_{t+1}-\left(L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t+1}\|-2L_{xx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}.

Applying
Lemma

A.1

(i)
on the term
\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t+1}\|
concludes the proof of the first part.

For the second part, the smoothness of
f_{t}
established in
Lemma

A.1

(v)
implies

\displaystyle f_{t}(x_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(x_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=f_{t}(x_{t})+(\nabla f_{t}(x_{t})-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}))^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=f_{t}(x_{t})+(\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}))^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+\|\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})\|\cdot\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+L_{yx}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|\cdot\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2},

where the second inequality follows from Cauchy-Schwartz inequality and the third inequality follows from the Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla_{x}{\cal L}
. By
Lemma

A.1

(vi)
, we have

\displaystyle f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})-f_{t}(x_{t+1})\leq\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\iff f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t+1}\leq f_{t}(x_{t+1})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t},

which together with applying
Lemma

A.1

(i)
on
\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|
implies the second part. This completes the proof.
∎

To implement single-loop strategies, we formally define two classes of oracle updates used in our algorithms.

Definition A.3

(LMO Sequences)
.

Suppose
\{x_{t},y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is a sequence in
{\cal X}\times{\cal Y}
.

\diamond

A sequence
\{x_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal X}
is a
primal LMO sequence
with stepsizes
\{\tau_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset[0,1]
if for any
t\geq 0

x_{t+1}=x_{t}+\tau_{t}(v_{t}-x_{t}),\ \text{where~}v_{t}=\LMO_{{\cal X}}(\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})).

(
15
a)

\diamond

A sequence
\{y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal Y}
is a
dual LMO sequence
if for any
t\geq 0
we have
\max\{\beta_{t},\mu\}>0
and

y_{t+1}=y_{t}+\gamma_{t}(u_{t}-y_{t}),\ \text{where~}u_{t}=\LMO_{{\cal Y}}(-\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{\beta_{t}}(x_{t},y_{t}))\ \text{and}\ \textstyle\gamma_{t}=\min\left\{\frac{\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{\beta_{t}}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}},1\right\}.

(
15
b)

The dual updates utilize the property that
{\cal L}_{t}(x,\cdot)
is
(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})
-smooth and concave under
Assumption

1.1
. The stepsize
\gamma_{t}
in the dual LMO sequence represents a
closed-loop strategy
that maximizes the quadratic lower bound of the objective.
Similarly, the dual PO update below uses a stepsize that maximizes the corresponding quadratic lower bound.

Definition A.4

(PO Sequences)
.

Suppose
\{x_{t},y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is a sequence in
{\cal X}\times{\cal Y}
.

\diamond

A sequence
\{x_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal X}
is a
primal PO sequence
with stepsizes
\{\tau_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset\mathbb{R}_{++}
if for any
t\geq 0

x_{t+1}=\PO_{{\cal X}}\left(x_{t}-\tau_{t}\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})\right).

(
15
c)

\diamond

A sequence
\{y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal Y}
is a
dual PO sequence
if for any
t\geq 0
we have
\max\{\beta_{t},\mu\}>0
and

y_{t+1}=\PO_{{\cal Y}}\left(y_{t}+\gamma_{t}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\right),\ \text{where}\ \gamma_{t}=1/(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}).

(
15
d)

The subsequent sections provide convergence results for every combination of these primal and dual updates to establish a unified analysis for our single-loop framework.

A.3
Primal LMO sequence

We characterize the iterate progress when the primal sequence is updated via an LMO according to
Definition

A.3
. The following lemma instantiates
Lemma

A.2
for the primal LMO update and establishes a recursive bound linking
H_{t}
with the regularized payoff and primal functions.

Lemma A.5
.

Suppose Assumption
1.1
holds. Let
\{x_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a primal LMO sequence with stepsizes
\{\tau_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
generated by the update rule (
15a
), let
\{y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal Y}
be any dual sequence, and let
\{\beta_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a non-increasing sequence of smoothing parameters such that
\max\{\beta_{t},\mu\}>0
. Then, for any
t\geq 0
,

{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\geq H_{t+1}-\frac{\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)}{\sqrt{\beta_{t+1}+\mu}}\sqrt{H_{t+1}}-2L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2},

and if additionally
\beta_{t}\geq\beta_{t+1}
then

\displaystyle f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t}+L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|+\tau_{t}\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(v_{t}-x_{t})+\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t}+L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}+\tau_{t}\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(v_{t}-x_{t})+\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}}{2}.

Proof.

The proof directly follows from
Lemma

A.2
by substituting the primal LMO update
x_{t+1}-x_{t}=\tau_{t}(v_{t}-x_{t})
and applying the bound
\|v_{t}-x_{t}\|\leq D_{{\cal X}}
. The details are omitted for brevity.
∎

The next lemma provides a bound on the LMO and dual gaps in terms of the
H_{t}
.

Lemma A.6
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma

A.5
, for any
t\geq 0
, the dual stationarity measure satisfies

G_{{\cal Y}}(x_{t},y_{t})\leq H_{t}+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}.

Furthermore, if the primal stepsizes satisfy
\tau_{t}\geq\tau_{t+1}
, the accumulated primal stationarity gap satisfies

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T}G_{{\cal X}}^{\LMO}(x_{t},y_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\max_{x\in{\cal X}}|f(x)|+\beta_{0}D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{\tau_{T}}+L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\max_{x\in{\cal X}}|f(x)|+\beta_{0}D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{\tau_{T}}+L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}.

Proof.

For any
y\in{\cal Y}
, we have

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y)-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})

\displaystyle={\cal L}(x_{t},y)-{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})-\frac{\beta_{t}}{2}\|y-y_{0}\|^{2}+\frac{\beta_{t}}{2}\|y_{t}-y_{0}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq{\cal L}(x_{t},y)-{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})-\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}.

By maximizing both sides over
y\in{\cal Y}
and using the definition of
H_{t}
, we obtain

G_{{\cal Y}}(x_{t},y_{t})=\max_{y\in{\cal Y}}{\cal L}(x_{t},y)-{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})\leq H_{t}+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}.

This concludes the proof of the first part.

Using
Lemma

A.5
, we have

\displaystyle f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t+1}-f_{t}(x_{t})-\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t}

\displaystyle\leq L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|+\tau_{t}\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(v_{t}-x_{t})+\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}}{2},

which rearranges to

\displaystyle G_{{\cal X}}^{\LMO}(x_{t},y_{t})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t}-v_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t}}{\tau_{t}}-\frac{f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t+1}}{\tau_{t}}+L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|+\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}.

Summing the above inequality for
t=0,\dots,T
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T}G_{{\cal X}}^{\LMO}(x_{t},y_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{t=0}^{T}\left(\frac{f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t}}{\tau_{t}}-\frac{f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t+1}}{\tau_{t}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{f_{0}(x_{0})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{0}}{\tau_{0}}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{t}}-\frac{1}{\tau_{t-1}}\right)\left(f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t}\right)-\frac{f_{T+1}(x_{T+1})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{T+1}}{\tau_{T}}

\displaystyle\quad+L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}.

For any
t\geq 0
, it holds from
Lemma

A.1

(vi)
that

f(x_{t})\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t}\leq f(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{0},

which implies

\left|f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t}\right|\leq\max_{x\in{\cal X}}\left|f(x)\right|+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{0}.

Since
\tau_{t}\leq\tau_{t-1}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T}G_{{\cal X}}^{\LMO}(x_{t},y_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\left(\max_{x\in{\cal X}}\left|f(x)\right|+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{0}\right)\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{t}}-\frac{1}{\tau_{t-1}}\right)+\frac{1}{\tau_{T}}\right)+L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{2\max_{x\in{\cal X}}\left|f(x)\right|+\beta_{0}D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{\tau_{T}}+L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}.

Applying the strong concavity property from
Lemma

A.1

(i)
completes the proof.
∎

The guarantees established in
Lemma

A.5
for the primal objective function can be further refined under the additional assumption that the payoff function is convex-concave.

Lemma A.7
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma

A.5
and the additional
Assumption

1.4
, for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})-f_{t+1}(x^{\star})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t+1}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\tau_{t})\left(f_{t}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x^{\star})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}\right)+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\tau_{t}\beta_{t}}{2}+2L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}\|y_{t}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|+\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\tau_{t})\left(f_{t}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x^{\star})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}\right)+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\tau_{t}\beta_{t}}{2}+2L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}+\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}^{2}}{2}.

Proof.

From the definition of
v_{t}
as the LMO solution, it holds that

\displaystyle\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(v_{t}-x_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x^{\star}-x_{t})

\displaystyle=\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))^{\top}(x^{\star}-x_{t})+(\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})))^{\top}(x^{\star}-x_{t}).

Under
Assumption

1.4
,
L(\cdot,y)
is convex, which implies that the regularized primal function
f_{t}
is also convex on
{\cal X}
. By the gradient property of smoothed functions in
Lemma

A.1

(v)
, we have

\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))^{\top}(x^{\star}-x_{t})=\nabla_{x}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))^{\top}(x^{\star}-x_{t})=\nabla f_{t}(x_{t})^{\top}(x^{\star}-x_{t})\leq f_{t}(x^{\star})-f_{t}(x_{t}).

Applying the Cauchy-Schwarz inequality and the Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla_{x}{\cal L}
yields

\displaystyle(\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})))^{\top}(x^{\star}-x_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\|\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))\|\|x^{\star}-x_{t}\|

\displaystyle\leq L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\|y_{t}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|.

where we used the fact that
\|x^{\star}-x_{t}\|\leq D_{{\cal X}}
. We thus obtain

\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(v_{t}-x_{t})\leq L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\|y_{t}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|+f_{t}(x^{\star})-f_{t}(x_{t}).

Substituting this bound into the primal progress result from
Lemma

A.5
leads to

f_{t}(x_{t+1})-f_{t}(x^{\star})\leq(1-\tau_{t})(f_{t}(x_{t})-f_{t}(x^{\star}))+2L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}\|y_{t}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|+\left(\frac{L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2\beta_{t}}\right)\tau_{t}^{2}.

(
16
)

From the properties of changing the smoothing parameter in
Lemma

A.1

(vi)
, we have

\displaystyle f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})-f_{t+1}(x^{\star})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(f_{t}(x_{t+1})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t}-\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t+1}\right)-f_{t}(x^{\star})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t+1}

\displaystyle=f_{t}(x_{t+1})-f_{t}(x^{\star})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\beta_{t},

Combining this with (
16
) and the strong concavity relation in
Lemma

A.1

(i)
completes the proof.
∎

A.4
Dual LMO sequence

We characterize the iterate progress when the dual sequence is updated via an LMO according to
Definition

A.3
, for which the stepsize is adaptive and follows a closed-loop strategy. The following lemma establishes a recursive bound linking
H_{t}
with the regularized payoff functions.

Lemma A.8
.

Suppose Assumption
1.1
holds. Let
\{x_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal X}
be any primal sequence and let
\{y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a dual LMO sequence generated by the update rule (
15b
). Then, for any
t\geq 0
,

{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\leq\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},1-\frac{H_{t}}{2(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}\right\}H_{t}.

(
17
)

Proof.

From the Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}
and the dual update rule, we have that

\displaystyle-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\leq-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})-\gamma_{t}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})+\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}\gamma_{t}^{2}.

(
18
)

If

\frac{\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}}\geq 1\iff\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})\geq(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2},

then the dual stepsize
\gamma_{t}=1
and

\displaystyle-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})-\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})+\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t}).

Otherwise,
\gamma_{t}=\tfrac{\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}}
, and we obtain

\displaystyle-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})-\frac{(\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t}))^{2}}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t}))^{2}}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})-\frac{1}{2}\frac{(\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t}))^{2}}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}}.

Combining these two cases yields

\displaystyle-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})-\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{1,\frac{\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}}\right\}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})

By the definition of
u_{t}
and the concavity of
{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},\cdot)
it holds that

\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})\geq\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t})\geq{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})=H_{t}\geq 0.

Applying this relation and the diameter bound
\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|\leq D_{{\cal Y}}
we deduce

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq H_{t}-\frac{1}{2}\min\left\{1,\frac{H_{t}}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}\right\}H_{t},

which concludes the proof.
∎

The guarantees established in
Lemma

A.8
for the regularized payoff function can be further refined under the additional assumption that the feasible set
{\cal Y}
is strongly convex.

Lemma A.9
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma

A.8
and the additional
Assumption

1.2
, for any
t\geq 0
,

{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\leq\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},1-\frac{\alpha\sqrt{(\beta_{t}+\mu)H_{t}}}{8\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)}\right\}H_{t}.

(
19
)

Proof.

Using inequality (
18
) and definition of
H_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq H_{t}-\gamma_{t}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})+\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}\gamma_{t}^{2}.

Since
\gamma_{t}
is chosen to minimize this quadratic upper bound, the following inequality remains valid for any
\gamma\in[0,1]

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq H_{t}-\gamma\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})+\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}\gamma^{2}.

(
20
)

To exploit the geometry of the constraint set, we define the point

p_{t}:=\frac{1}{2}(y_{t}+u_{t})+\frac{\alpha}{8}\|y_{t}-u_{t}\|^{2}w_{t},

where
w_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{n_{y}}
satisfies
w_{t}^{\top}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})=\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\|
. By construction and
Assumption

1.2
, we have
p_{t}\in{\cal Y}
. Besides, by the optimality of
u_{t}
for the dual LMO update, it holds that

\displaystyle\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})

\displaystyle\geq\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(p_{t}-y_{t})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})+\frac{\alpha}{8}\|y_{t}-u_{t}\|^{2}\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t})+\frac{\alpha}{8}\|y_{t}-u_{t}\|^{2}\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\|

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{2}H_{t}+\frac{\alpha}{8}\|y_{t}-u_{t}\|^{2}\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\|,

where the last inequality follows from concavity of
{\cal L}(x_{t},\cdot)
and the definition of
H_{t}
.
Using the above lower bound and plugging it in (
20
), we may conclude that

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)H_{t}+\frac{\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}}{2}\left(\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)\gamma^{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\|\gamma\right).

If
\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)<\frac{\alpha}{4}\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\|
, then we choose
\gamma=1
and hence,

{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\leq\frac{1}{2}H_{t}.

Otherwise, we choose
\gamma=\frac{\alpha}{4\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)}\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\|
and hence,

{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\leq\left(1-\frac{\alpha\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\|}{8\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)}\right)H_{t}.

Applying the strong concavity property from
Lemma

A.1

(i)
, which implies
\|\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\|\geq\sqrt{2(\beta_{t}+\mu)H_{t}}
, we obtain the desired bound.
∎

A.5
Primal PO sequence

Under the primal PO sequence, the stationarity measure is characterized by

G_{{\cal X}}^{\PO}
as defined in (
4b
) with
\sigma=\tau_{0}
:

G_{\mathcal{X}}^{\PO}(x_{t},y_{t})=\left\|\frac{1}{\tau_{0}}\left(\PO(x_{t}-\tau_{0}\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}))-x_{t}\right)\right\|\geq\left\|\frac{1}{\tau_{t}}\left(\PO(x_{t}-\tau_{t}\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}))-x_{t}\right)\right\|=\left\|\frac{x_{t+1}-x_{t}}{\tau_{t}}\right\|,

where the inequality follows from
Beck (2017, Theorem 10.9)
when
\tau_{0}\geq\tau_{t}
.
The following lemma establishes a fundamental inequality for this sequence.

Lemma A.10
.

Under
Assumption

1.1
, let
\{x_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a primal PO sequence with stepsizes
\{\tau_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
generated by the update rule (
15c
), let
\{y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal Y}
be any dual sequence, and let
\{\beta_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a non-increasing sequence of smoothing parameters such that
\max\{\beta_{t},\mu\}>0
. Then, for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\left(\frac{3}{4\tau_{t}}-\frac{L_{xx}}{2}-\frac{5L_{yx}^{2}}{2(\beta_{t}+\mu)}\right)\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}\right)-\left(f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t+1}}{2}\right)+\frac{1}{4}\min\left\{H_{t},\frac{H_{t}^{2}}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}\right\}+\frac{4L_{yx}^{4}(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}}{(\beta_{t}+\mu)^{2}}.

Proof.

For any
x\in{\cal X}
, the first-order optimality condition for the projection step implies

\left(x_{t+1}-x_{t}+\tau_{t}\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})\right)^{\top}(x-x_{t+1})\geq 0.

Setting
x=x_{t}
yields the following bound on the linearized gradient

\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})\leq-\frac{1}{\tau_{t}}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}.

Applying
Lemma

A.2
and the strong concavity relation
\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|\leq\sqrt{2H_{t}/(\beta_{t}+\mu)}
, we have

\displaystyle f_{t+1}(x_{t+1})

\displaystyle+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t+1}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}+L_{yx}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|-\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{t}}-\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\right)\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}+L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}-\left(\frac{1}{\tau_{t}}-\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\right)\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}.

(
21
)

To handle the interaction term, we apply the AM-GM inequality in two ways. First, using the inequality for four numbers, we have

\displaystyle L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}L_{yx}(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{1/4}D_{{\cal Y}}^{1/2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|}{\sqrt{\beta_{t}+\mu}}\right)^{1/3}\right)^{3}\sqrt{\frac{H_{t}}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{1/2}D_{{\cal Y}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{H_{t}^{2}}{4(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}+\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}L_{yx}(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{1/4}D_{{\cal Y}}^{1/2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|}{\sqrt{\beta_{t}+\mu}}\right)^{4/3}.

Second, by the standard AM-GM inequality for two numbers, we obtain

\frac{2L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|}{\sqrt{\beta_{t}+\mu}}\sqrt{\frac{H_{t}}{2}}\leq\frac{H_{t}}{4}+\frac{2L_{yx}^{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}.

Combining these two bounds, the interaction term is bounded as follows

\displaystyle L_{yx}\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\min\left\{H_{t},\frac{H_{t}^{2}}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}\right\}+\frac{2L_{yx}^{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{3}{4}\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}L_{yx}(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{1/4}D_{{\cal Y}}^{1/2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|}{\sqrt{\beta_{t}+\mu}}\right)^{4/3}.

Further, we apply the AM-GM inequality for three numbers to the last term in the above inequality to get

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sqrt{2}L_{yx}(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{1/4}D_{{\cal Y}}^{1/2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|}{\sqrt{\beta_{t}+\mu}}\right)^{4/3}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\frac{\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|}{\sqrt{2\tau_{t}}}\right)\right)^{4/3}\left(\frac{2L_{yx}(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{1/4}\sqrt{D_{{\cal Y}}\tau_{t}}}{\sqrt{\beta_{t}+\mu}}\right)^{4/3}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\frac{\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|}{\sqrt{2\tau_{t}}}\right)^{2/3}\right)^{2}\left(\frac{2L_{yx}(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{1/4}\sqrt{D_{{\cal Y}}\tau_{t}}}{\sqrt{\beta_{t}+\mu}}\right)^{4/3}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2}{3}\frac{\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}}{2\tau_{t}}+\frac{1}{3}\frac{16L_{yx}^{4}(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}}{(\beta_{t}+\mu)^{2}},

which implies

\displaystyle L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{4}\min\left\{H_{t},\frac{H_{t}^{2}}{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}\right\}+\left(\frac{1}{4\tau_{t}}+\frac{2L_{yx}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4L_{yx}^{4}(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}}{(\beta_{t}+\mu)^{2}}.

Substituting this bound into (
21
) and using the definition of the primal smoothness modulus
L_{f_{t}}=L_{xx}+{L_{yx}^{2}}/{(\beta_{t}+\mu)}
completes the proof.
∎

Under the additional assumption of convexity from
Assumption

1.4
, we establish the convergence of the primal suboptimality gap in the following lemma.

Lemma A.11
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma

A.10
and the additional
Assumption

1.4
, let
\{\tau_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a stepsize sequence satisfying
0<\tau_{t}<1/L_{f_{t}}
. Then, for any
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}f(x_{t+1})-f(x^{\star})\leq\left(1+\sup_{t\geq 0}\frac{L_{yx}^{2}\tau_{t}}{\left(1-L_{f_{t}}\tau_{t}\right)(\beta_{t}+\mu)}\right)\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}H_{t}+\frac{D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2(T+1)\tau_{T}}+\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2(T+1)}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\beta_{t}.

Proof.

Recalling that
H_{t}=f_{t}(x_{t})-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})
and using the
L_{f_{t}}
-smoothness of
f_{t}
, we have

\displaystyle f_{t}(x_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq f_{t}(x_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(x_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=H_{t}+{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})+\nabla f_{t}(x_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}.

By the convexity of
{\cal L}_{t}(\cdot,y_{t})
, we can link the current iterate to the optimizer
x^{\star}
as follows

\displaystyle f_{t}(x_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq H_{t}+{\cal L}_{t}(x^{\star},y_{t})+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t}-x^{\star})+\nabla f_{t}(x_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle=H_{t}+{\cal L}_{t}(x^{\star},y_{t})+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t}-x_{t+1}+x_{t+1}-x^{\star})+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x_{t})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq H_{t}+f_{t}(x^{\star})+\|\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})\|\cdot\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x^{\star})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2},

where the last inequality follows from the definition of
f_{t}
and Cauchy-Schwartz inequality. By
Lemma

A.1

(vi)
, we have
f_{t}(x)\leq f(x)\leq f_{t}(x)+{\cal D}_{\cal Y}^{2}\beta_{t}/{2}
, implying
f(x_{t+1})-f(x^{\star})-{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}/{2}\leq f_{t}(x_{t+1})-f_{t}(x^{\star})
. This yields

\displaystyle f(x_{t+1})-f(x^{\star})-\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq H_{t}+\|\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})\|\cdot\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|+\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x^{\star})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}.

The first-order optimality condition for the projection step at
x_{t+1}
implies

(x_{t+1}-x_{t}+\tau_{t}\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}))^{\top}(x-x_{t+1})\geq 0,\quad\forall x\in{\cal X},

which yields the standard three-point relation

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\|x_{t}-x^{\star}\|^{2}-\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|^{2}-\|x_{t+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2})

\displaystyle=(x_{t+1}-x_{t})^{\top}(x^{\star}-x_{t+1})

\displaystyle\geq\tau_{t}\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t+1}-x^{\star}).

Using this bound, we arrive at

\displaystyle f(x_{t+1})-f(x^{\star})-\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq H_{t}+\|\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})\|\cdot\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{2\tau_{t}}(\|x_{t}-x^{\star}\|^{2}-\|x_{t}-x_{t+1}\|^{2}-\|x_{t+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2})+\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}.

Using this Lipschitz smooth of
\nabla_{x}{\cal L}
and the strong concavity relation
\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|\leq\sqrt{2H_{t}/(\beta_{t}+\mu)}
, we have

\displaystyle f(x_{t+1})-f(x^{\star})-\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq H_{t}+L_{yx}\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|-\left(\frac{1}{2\tau_{t}}-\frac{L_{f_{t}}}{2}\right)\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|^{2}+\frac{\|x_{t}-x^{\star}\|^{2}-\|x_{t+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}}{2\tau_{t}}

\displaystyle\leq H_{t}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}\tau_{t}H_{t}}{\left(1-L_{f_{t}}\tau_{t}\right)(\beta_{t}+\mu)}+\frac{\|x_{t}-x^{\star}\|^{2}-\|x_{t+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}}{2\tau_{t}},

where the last inequality follows from the fact that
ax-\frac{bx^{2}}{2}\leq\frac{a^{2}}{2b}
for any
x,a,b>0
. As a result,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T}\left(f(x_{t+1})-f(x^{\star})-\frac{D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\beta_{t}}{2}\right)\leq\left(1+\sup_{t\geq 0}\frac{L_{yx}^{2}\tau_{t}}{\left(1-L_{f_{t}}\tau_{t}\right)(\beta_{t}+\mu)}\right)\sum_{t=0}^{T}H_{t}+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{\|x_{t}-x^{\star}\|^{2}-\|x_{t+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}}{2\tau_{t}}.

Observing that

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{\|x_{t}-x^{\star}\|^{2}-\|x_{t+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}}{2\tau_{t}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\|x_{0}-x^{\star}\|^{2}}{2\tau_{0}}+\sum_{t\in[T]}\left(\frac{1}{2\tau_{t}}-\frac{1}{2\tau_{t-1}}\right)\|x_{t}-x^{\star}\|^{2}-\frac{\|x_{T+1}-x^{\star}\|^{2}}{2\tau_{T}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2\tau_{0}}+\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(\frac{1}{2\tau_{t}}-\frac{1}{2\tau_{t-1}}\right)D_{{\cal X}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2\tau_{T}}

together with a simple rearrangement conclude the proof.
∎

A.6
Dual PO sequence

We conclude this section by analyzing the dual PO update rule, for which the stepsize is adaptive and follows a closed-loop strategy. The following lemma provides a standard result established by viewing the update for
y_{t+1}
as a step of the proximal gradient method on the
(\beta_{t}+\mu)
-strongly convex function
-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},\cdot)
, which has a Lipschitz gradient modulus
L_{yy}+\beta_{t}
and a unique minimizer
y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})
.

Lemma A.12
.

Under
Assumption

1.1
, let
\{x_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subseteq{\cal X}
be any primal sequence, let
\{y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be dual PO sequence generated by the update rule (
15d
), and let
\{\beta_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a non-increasing sequence of smoothing parameters such that
\max\{\beta_{t},\mu\}>0
. Then, for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{yy}-\mu}{2}\|y_{t}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|^{2}-\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|y_{t+1}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|^{2}.

Proof.

By the
(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})
-Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},\cdot)
, we have

-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\leq-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})-\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(y_{t+1}-y_{t})+\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|y_{t+1}-y_{t}\|^{2}.

By the
(\beta_{t}+\mu)
-strong concavity of
{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},\cdot)
, it holds that

{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))\leq{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})+\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t})-\frac{\beta_{t}+\mu}{2}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|^{2}.

Summing these two inequalities yields

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\\
&\leq\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t+1})+\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|y_{t+1}-y_{t}\|^{2}-\frac{\beta_{t}+\mu}{2}\|y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}\|^{2}.\end{aligned}

(
22
)

By the definition of the PO update rule with stepsize
\gamma_{t}
, the following first-order optimality condition for the projection step at
y_{t+1}
is satisfied

\left(y_{t+1}-y_{t}-\frac{1}{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})\right)^{\top}(y-y_{t+1})\geq 0,\quad\forall y\in{\cal Y},

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\|y_{t}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|^{2}-\|y_{t+1}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|^{2}-\|y_{t+1}-y_{t}\|^{2})

\displaystyle=(y_{t}-y_{t+1})^{\top}(y_{t+1}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t+1}).

Multiplying by
L_{yy}+\beta_{t}
and substituting this bound into (
22
) will conclude the proof.
∎

The following lemma establishes a recursive bound linking
H_{t}
with the regularized payoff functions.

Lemma A.13
.

Under the assumptions of
Lemma

A.12
, for any
t\geq 0
,

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{yy}-\mu}{2}\|y_{t}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|^{2}-\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|y_{t+1}-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})\left(\frac{L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|+2D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})}{\beta_{t+1}+\mu}\right)\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t+1}}{\beta_{t+1}+\mu}}.

Proof.

Observing

\displaystyle\|y_{t+1}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|^{2}

\displaystyle=\|y_{t+1}-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})+y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\geq\|y_{t+1}-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})\|^{2}+2(y_{t+1}-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))^{\top}(y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))

together with
Lemma

A.12
imply that

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{yy}-\mu}{2}\|y_{t}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|^{2}-\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|y_{t+1}-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad-(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})(y_{t+1}-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1}))^{\top}(y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{yy}-\mu}{2}\|y_{t}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|^{2}-\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|y_{t+1}-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})\|y_{t+1}-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})\|\cdot\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|.

Observe next that

\displaystyle\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|

\displaystyle\leq\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|+\|y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t})\|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})}{\beta_{t+1}+\mu}+\frac{L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|}{\beta_{t+1}+\mu},

where the inequality follows from
Lemma

F.5
and
Lemma

A.1

(iii)
.
We thus deduce that

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{yy}-\mu}{2}\|y_{t}-y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})\|^{2}-\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|y_{t+1}-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})\|^{2}

\displaystyle\quad+(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})(\|y_{t+1}-y_{t+1}^{\star}(x_{t+1})\|)\left(\frac{L_{yx}\|x_{t+1}-x_{t}\|+2D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})}{\beta_{t+1}+\mu}\right).

Applying
Lemma

A.1

(i)
will conclude the proof.
∎

Appendix B
Proofs of
Section

1

We provide the formal proofs for the relationships between the stationarity and sub-optimality metrics discussed in
Section

1.2.1
. The following result restates
Lemma

1.3
from the main text in a more comprehensive form, detailing both directions of the inequality between the LMO and PO gap functions.

Lemma B.1
.

If
Assumption

1.1
holds then for any
(x,y)\in{\cal X}\times{\cal Y}
and
\sigma>0
, it holds that

\displaystyle G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sigma\|\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)\|+D_{{\cal X}}\right)G_{\cal X}^{\PO}(x,y),

\displaystyle G_{\cal X}^{\PO}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\frac{G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x,y)}{\sigma}}.

Proof.

We begin with the first inequality. Let the projected point be denoted as

\displaystyle\bar{x}:=\PO_{{\cal X}}\left(x-\sigma\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)\right).

By the definition of the primal PO stationarity measure, we have
\|\bar{x}-x\|=\sigma G_{\cal X}^{\PO}(x,y)
. From the first-order optimality condition of the projection onto the convex set
{\cal X}
, for any
v\in{\cal X}
it holds that

\left(\bar{x}-x+\sigma\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)\right)^{\top}(v-\bar{x})\geq 0\quad\iff\quad\sigma\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)^{\top}(\bar{x}-v)\leq(\bar{x}-x)^{\top}(v-\bar{x}).

(
23
)

Therefore, we may conclude that

\displaystyle\sigma\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)^{\top}(x-v)

\displaystyle=\sigma\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)^{\top}(x-\bar{x})+\sigma\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)^{\top}(\bar{x}-v)

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)^{\top}(x-\bar{x})+(\bar{x}-x)^{\top}(v-\bar{x})

\displaystyle\leq\sigma\|\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)\|\cdot\|x-\bar{x}\|+\|v-\bar{x}\|\cdot\|\bar{x}-x\|

\displaystyle\leq\left(\sigma\|\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)\|+D_{{\cal X}}\right)\sigma G_{\cal X}^{\PO}(x,y),

where the first inequality follows from (
23
), the second inequality follows from the Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, and last inequality follows from the compactness of
{\cal X}
.
Dividing both sides by
\sigma
and taking the maximum over
v\in{\cal X}
yields the first claimed inequality.

To establish the second inequality, we set
v=x
in the optimality condition (
23
), which results in

\displaystyle\sigma\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)^{\top}(\bar{x}-x)\leq(\bar{x}-x)^{\top}(x-\bar{x})\quad\iff\quad\|\bar{x}-x\|^{2}\leq\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)^{\top}(x-\bar{x}).

Given that
\bar{x}\in{\cal X}
the term on the right-hand side is bounded by the LMO gap,
\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x,y)^{\top}(x-\bar{x})\leq G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x,y)
. Recalling that
G_{\cal X}^{\PO}(x,y)=\|\bar{x}-x\|/\sigma
, we deduce

(\sigma G_{\cal X}^{\PO}(x,y))^{2}\leq\sigma G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x,y)\iff G_{\cal X}^{\PO}(x,y)\leq\sqrt{\frac{G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x,y)}{\sigma}},

which completes the proof.
∎

We now provide the proof for
Lemma

1.5
, which relies on the convexity-concavity of the payoff function
L
and the application of Jensen’s inequality to the average iterates.

Lemma B.2
.

Suppose
\{x_{t},y_{t}\}_{t=1}^{T}
is any sequence in
{\cal X}\times{\cal Y}
. If
Assumptions

1.1
and
1.4
hold, then we have

\displaystyle G_{{\cal X},{\cal Y}}^{\Dual}(\bar{x}_{T},\bar{y}_{T})\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x_{t},y_{t})+G_{\cal Y}(x_{t},y_{t})\right),

where
\bar{x}_{T}:=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}x_{t}
and
\bar{y}_{T}:=\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}y_{t}
denote the average iterates.

Proof.

For any arbitrary pair
(x,y)\in{\cal X}\times{\cal Y}
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{t\in[T]}\left(G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x_{t},y_{t})+G_{\cal Y}(x_{t},y_{t})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t\in[T]}\left(\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(x_{t}-x)+{\cal L}(x_{t},y)-{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})\right)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{t\in[T]}\left({\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})-{\cal L}(x,y_{t})+{\cal L}(x_{t},y)-{\cal L}(x_{t},y_{t})\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{t\in[T]}{\cal L}(x_{t},y)-\sum_{t\in[T]}{\cal L}(x,y_{t})

\displaystyle\geq T\left({\cal L}(\bar{x}_{T},y)-{\cal L}(x,\bar{y}_{T})\right).

where the first inequality follows from the definition of the LMO and dual gaps, the second follows from the first-order characterization of convexity for
{\cal L}(\cdot,y_{t})
, and the final inequality results from the application of Jensen’s inequality to the convex function
{\cal L}(\cdot,y)
and the concave function
{\cal L}(x,\cdot)
. Dividing both sides by
T
and taking the supremum over
y\in{\cal Y}
and the infimum over
x\in{\cal X}
yields

\max_{y\in{\cal Y}}{\cal L}(\bar{x}_{T},y)-\min_{x\in{\cal X}}{\cal L}(x,\bar{y}_{T})\leq\frac{1}{T}\sum_{t=1}^{T}\left(G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x_{t},y_{t})+G_{\cal Y}(x_{t},y_{t})\right).

Recalling the definition of
G_{{\cal X},{\cal Y}}^{\Dual}(\bar{x}_{T},\bar{y}_{T})
, we conclude the proof.
∎

Remark B.3

(Relationship to Game Stationarity and Oracle Metrics)
.

To provide a unified perspective on the convergence of our algorithms, we relate our stationarity measures to the standard notion of
\epsilon
-game stationarity
. A point
(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in{\cal X}\times{\cal Y}
is considered
\epsilon
-game stationary for the minimax problem (
1
) if it satisfies the following inclusion relations

-\nabla_{x}{\cal L}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in{\cal N}_{{\cal X}}(\bar{x})+{\cal B}(\epsilon)\quad\text{and}\quad\nabla_{y}{\cal L}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\in{\cal N}_{{\cal Y}}(\bar{y})+{\cal B}(\epsilon),

(
24
)

where
{\cal N}_{{\cal S}}(s)
denotes the normal cone to the set
{\cal S}
at point
s
, and
{\cal B}(\epsilon)
represents the Euclidean ball of radius
O(\sqrt{\epsilon})
. The justification for the gap functions used in this work is that an
\epsilon
-solution in terms of
G_{{\cal X}}^{\LMO}/G_{{\cal X}}^{\PO}
and
G_{{\cal Y}}
implies an
\epsilon
-game stationary solution. This relationship is justified as follows.

For the dual variable
y
, the condition
G_{{\cal Y}}(\bar{x},\bar{y})\leq\epsilon
implies that
(\bar{x},\bar{y})
is an
\epsilon
-approximate Nash equilibrium
for the
y
-player. This means the player cannot improve the payoff by more than
\epsilon
by deviating from
\bar{y}
. Furthermore, in the strongly concave settings (
\mu>0
), a bound on
G_{{\cal Y}}
directly implies the dual gradient inclusion in (
24
) via standard strong concavity inequalities.
For the primal variable
x
, the stationarity requirement (
24
) is satisfied if
G_{{\cal X}}^{\LMO}\leq\epsilon
as established in the projection-free literature
(
Boroun et al., 2023
)
. Crucially, for our hybrid algorithms that utilize a projection oracle,
Lemma

1.3
provides the necessary link. Specifically, the inequality
G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}\leq\left(\sigma\|\nabla_{x}L\|+D_{{\cal X}}\right)G_{\cal X}^{\PO}
ensures that a bound on the primal PO gap automatically establishes a bound on the LMO gap up to a constant factor. Therefore, an
\epsilon
-convergence rate in the PO gap implies the primal inclusion in (
24
). Consequently, our unified analysis across all oracle configurations provides a rigorous guarantee for reaching an
\epsilon
-game stationary solution.

Appendix C
Proofs of
Section

2

In this section we provide the detailed convergence analysis for
Algorithm

1
. While the algorithm shares its fundamental structure with
R-PDCG

(
Boroun et al., 2023
, Algorithm 1)
, our analysis shows that the choice of novel parameter settings leads to stronger convergence guarantees. Specifically, we adopt the following
anytime
schedules for the stepsize and regularization parameters.

Condition C.1
.

The sequences
\{\tau_{t},\beta_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
are chosen such that

\tau_{t}=(t+1)^{-a},\quad\beta_{t}=C(t+1)^{-b},

for constants
a,b\in(0,1]
and
C\geq 0
satisfying
\max\{C,\mu\}>0
, where
\mu
is the strong concavity modulus from
Assumption

1.1

(ii)
.

The requirement for
C
ensures that the framework remains flexible. If
{\cal L}(x,\cdot)
is already
\mu
-strongly concave for all
x\in{\cal X}
(i.e.,
Assumption

1.1

(ii)
holds with
\mu>0
), explicit smoothing is unnecessary, and we may set
C=0
.
By leveraging the recursive relationships established in
Lemma

A.5
and
Lemma

A.8
, we derive a technical recursion for the discrepancy sequence
\{H_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
. The following result establishes an explicit
non-asymptotic decay bound
for this sequence in terms of the iteration index
t
.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
\{x_{t},y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the sequence generated by
Algorithm

1
under parameters
\{\tau_{t},\beta_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
satisfying
Condition

C.1
with
2a>b
. Under
Assumption

1.1
, there exists a constant
M_{1}>0
such that for any
t\geq 0

H_{t}\leq\frac{M_{1}}{(t+1)^{(2a-b)/3}}.

If, in addition,
\mu>0
and
C=0
, there exists a constant
M_{2}>0
such that for any
t\geq 0

H_{t}\leq M_{2}\cdot\begin{cases}\displaystyle(t+1)^{-2a/3}&\displaystyle\text{if }a\in(0,1),\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\frac{\log(t+2)}{(t+1)^{2/3}}&\displaystyle\text{if }a=1.\end{cases}

The constants
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
depend only on the parameters
\{L_{xx},L_{yx},L_{yy},C,\mu,D_{{\cal X}},D_{{\cal Y}},a,b\}
and the initial iterates
\{H_{0},\dots,H_{4}\}
.

Proof.

From the Lipschitz continuity of
\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}
, we have

-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\leq-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})-\gamma_{t}\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})+\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}\gamma_{t}^{2}.

Since the adaptive stepsize
\gamma_{t}
, defined in (
15b
), minimizes the right-hand side over the interval
[0,1]
, the inequality holds for any arbitrary
\gamma\in[0,1]
. By adding
{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))
to both sides and using the definition of
H_{t}
, we obtain

\displaystyle{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\leq H_{t}-\gamma\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})+\frac{L_{yy}+\beta_{t}}{2}\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|^{2}\gamma^{2}.

(
25
)

From the definition of
u_{t}
as the dual LMO solution and the concavity of
{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},\cdot)
, we have

\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(u_{t}-y_{t})\geq\nabla_{y}{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})^{\top}(y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t})-y_{t})\geq{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t})=H_{t}\geq 0.

Using this observation, the fact that
\|u_{t}-y_{t}\|\leq D_{\cal Y}
and the inequality (
25
), we deduce

{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t}^{\star}(x_{t}))-{\cal L}_{t}(x_{t},y_{t+1})\leq(1-\gamma)H_{t}+\frac{(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})D_{\cal Y}^{2}}{2}\gamma^{2}.

Thanks to
Lemma

A.5
and the above inequality, we arrive at the following recursion

\displaystyle H_{t+1}-\frac{\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)}{\sqrt{\beta_{t+1}+\mu}}\sqrt{H_{t+1}}\leq(1-\gamma)H_{t}+\frac{\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)D^{2}}{2}\gamma^{2}+2L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}.

By applying the AM-GM inequality to the cross term involving
\sqrt{H_{t+1}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{\cal Y}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)}{\sqrt{\beta_{t+1}+\mu}}\sqrt{H_{t+1}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{\cal Y}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)}{\sqrt{\gamma(\beta_{t+1}+\mu)}}\sqrt{\gamma H_{t+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\gamma}{2}H_{t+1}+\frac{\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{\cal Y}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)^{2}}{\gamma(\beta_{t+1}+\mu)}.

(
26
)

Combining these expressions and multiplying the last term in the above inequality by two (to avoid introducing irrational constants in subsequent bounds), we arrive at the recursion

\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)H_{t+1}\leq(1-\gamma)H_{t}+\frac{\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2}\gamma^{2}+2L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}+\frac{2\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)^{2}}{\gamma(\beta_{t+1}+\mu)}.

Restricting
\gamma\in(0,1]
, we have
1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\in\left[\frac{1}{2},1\right)
and
\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\gamma/2}=1-\frac{\gamma}{2-\gamma}\leq 1-\frac{\gamma}{2}
. Hence, we have

\displaystyle H_{t+1}\leq\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)H_{t}+\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\gamma^{2}+4L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}+\frac{4\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)^{2}}{\gamma(\beta_{t+1}+\mu)},

which implies

\displaystyle\frac{H_{t+1}}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t+1}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{H_{t}}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}}+\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t+1}}\left(\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\gamma^{2}+4L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}+\frac{4\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)^{2}}{\gamma(\beta_{t+1}+\mu)}\right).

Using this recursively, for any
t\geq 1
, we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{H_{t}}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}}

\displaystyle\leq H_{0}\!+\!\sum_{i\in[t]}\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{i}}\left(\left(L_{yy}\!+\!\beta_{i-1}\right)D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\gamma^{2}\!+\!4L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{i-1}^{2}\!+\!\frac{4\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{i-1}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{i-1}-\beta_{i})\right)^{2}}{\gamma(\beta_{i}+\mu)}\right).

(
27
)

Using the requirements in
Condition

C.1
, we observe that

\displaystyle 4L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{i-1}^{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{4L_{xx}\max\{C,\mu\}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\max\{C,\mu\}i^{2a}}\leq\frac{8L_{xx}\max\{C,\mu\}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{Ci^{2a}+\mu i^{2a}}\leq\frac{8L_{xx}\max\{C,\mu\}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{Ci^{2a-b}+\mu i^{2a}}.

Moreover, we also have

\displaystyle\frac{4\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{i-1}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{i-1}-\beta_{i})\right)^{2}}{\gamma(\beta_{i}+\mu)}

\displaystyle=\frac{4\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}i^{-a}+CD_{{\cal Y}}(i^{-b}-(i+1)^{-b}))\right)^{2}}{\gamma(Ci^{-b}+\mu)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}i^{-2a}+8(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}(i^{-b}-(i+1)^{-b})^{2}}{\gamma(Ci^{-b}+\mu)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}i^{-2a}+8b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}i^{-2(b+1)}}{\gamma(Ci^{-b}+\mu)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{8\left((L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)i^{-2a}}{\gamma(Ci^{-b}+\mu)},

(
28
)

where the first inequality follows from
(x+y)^{2}\leq 2(x^{2}+y^{2})
, the second inequality follows from
Lemma

F.2
, and the last inequality follows as
b+1>1\geq a
. Thus, we can further upper bound the last term in (
27
) as follows

\displaystyle\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\gamma^{2}+4L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}+\frac{4\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)^{2}}{\gamma(\beta_{t+1}+\mu)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(L_{yy}+C\right)D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\gamma^{2}+\frac{8L_{xx}\max\{C,\mu\}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{Ci^{2a-b}+\mu i^{2a}}+\frac{8\left((L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)i^{-2a}}{\gamma(Ci^{-b}+\mu)}

\displaystyle\leq\left(L_{yy}+C\right)D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\gamma^{2}+\frac{8\left(L_{xx}\max\{C,\mu\}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{\gamma(Ci^{2a-b}+\mu i^{2a})}.

Using the fact that

\sum_{i\in[t]}\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{i}}=\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)}\frac{\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}}-1}{\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)}-1}=\frac{2}{\gamma}\left(\frac{1}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}}-1\right)\leq\frac{2}{\gamma\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}}

and the recursion (
27
), we have

\displaystyle\frac{H_{t}}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}}

\displaystyle\leq H_{0}+\frac{2\left(L_{yy}+C\right)D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\gamma}{\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}}+\sum_{i\in[t]}\frac{8\left(L_{xx}\max\{C,\mu\}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{\gamma\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{i}(Ci^{2a-b}+\mu i^{2a})}.

Using Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, one can easily show that

\displaystyle\left(\frac{C}{i^{2a-b}}+\frac{\mu}{i^{2a}}\right)\left(Ci^{2a-b}+\mu i^{2a}\right)\geq(C+\mu)^{2}\implies\frac{(C+\mu)^{2}}{Ci^{2a-b}+\mu i^{2a}}\leq\left(\frac{C}{i^{2a-b}}+\frac{\mu}{i^{2a}}\right).

(
29
)

Thus, the following recursion holds

\displaystyle H_{t}

\displaystyle\leq H_{0}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}+2\left(L_{yy}+C\right)D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\gamma

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{1}{\gamma}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}\sum_{i\in[t]}\frac{8\left(L_{xx}\max\{C,\mu\}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{(C+\mu)^{2}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{i}}\left(\frac{C}{i^{2a-b}}+\frac{\mu}{i^{2a}}\right).

Applying
Lemma

F.4
, for any
t\geq 1
such that
t-\left\lfloor\frac{2}{\gamma}\right\rfloor\geq 0
, we obtain the following recursion

\displaystyle H_{t}

\displaystyle\leq H_{0}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}+2\left(L_{yy}+C\right)D_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\gamma

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{16\left(L_{xx}\max\{C,\mu\}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{(C+\mu)\gamma^{2}}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t-\left\lfloor\frac{2}{\gamma}\right\rfloor+1}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{64\left(L_{xx}\max\{C,\mu\}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{(C+\mu)^{2}\gamma^{2}}\left(\frac{CE(a,b))}{t^{2a-b}}+\mu\begin{cases}\frac{E(a,0)}{t^{2a}}&0<a<1\\
\frac{\log(t)}{t^{2}}&a=1\end{cases}\right),

where
E(a,b)
is defined in
Lemma

F.4
.
Observe that

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\gamma t}{2}\right)\quad\text{and}\quad\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t-\left\lfloor\frac{2}{\gamma}\right\rfloor+1}\leq\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t-\frac{2}{\gamma}}\leq\exp\left(-\frac{\gamma t}{2}+1\right),

(
30
)

where in the last inequality, we use the definition of floor function. If
\mu=0
, setting
\gamma=t^{-(2a-b)/3}
yields

\displaystyle H_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\left(H_{0}+\frac{16\left(L_{xx}CD_{{\cal X}}^{2}+(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)\exp(1)}{C}t^{(4a-2b)/3}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{t^{(3-2a+b)/3}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{2C\left(L_{yy}+C\right)D^{2}+64E(a,b)\left(L_{xx}CD_{{\cal X}}^{2}+(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{Ct^{(2a-b)/3}},

On the other hand, if
\mu>0
, setting
\gamma=t^{-2a/3}
yields

\displaystyle H_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\left(H_{0}+\frac{16\left(L_{xx}\mu D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}\right)e}{\mu}t^{4a/3}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{t^{(3-2a)/3}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{4L_{yy}D^{2}}{t^{2a/3}}+\frac{16\left(L_{xx}\mu D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}\right)}{\mu}\begin{cases}\frac{E(a,0)}{t^{2a/3}},&0<a<1\\
\frac{\log(t)}{t^{2/3}},&a=1.\end{cases}

Recall that applying
Lemma

F.4
requires
t-\left\lfloor\frac{2}{\gamma}\right\rfloor\geq 0
. With our choices of
\gamma
, this condition reduces to
t-\left\lfloor 2t^{2/3}\right\rfloor\geq 0
, which holds for all
t\geq 5
. Therefore, the existence of such
M_{1}
and
M_{2}
is guaranteed. This competes the proof.
∎

The guarantees established in
Lemma

C.2
can be further refined when the maximization domain
{\cal Y}
is strongly convex.

Lemma C.3
.

Let
\{x_{t},y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be the sequence generated by
Algorithm

1
under parameters
\{\tau_{t},\beta_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
satisfying
Condition

C.1
with
2a>b
. Under
Assumptions

1.1
and
1.2
, there exists a constant
M_{3}>0
such that for any
t\geq 0

H_{t}\leq\frac{M_{3}}{(t+1)^{a-b}}.

If, in addition,
\mu>0
and
C=0
, there exists a constant
M_{4}>0
such that for any
t\geq 0

H_{t}\leq M_{4}\cdot\begin{cases}\displaystyle(t+1)^{-a}&\displaystyle\text{if }a\in(0,1),\\[4.30554pt]
\displaystyle\frac{\log(t+2)}{t+1}&\displaystyle\text{if }a=1.\end{cases}

The constants
M_{3}
and
M_{4}
depend only on the parameters
\{L_{xx},L_{yx},L_{yy},C,\mu,D_{{\cal X}},D_{{\cal Y}},a,b\}
and the initial iterates
\{H_{0},\dots,H_{13}\}
.

Proof.

Applying
Lemma

A.5
and
Lemma

A.9
, we obtain the following recursion

\displaystyle H_{t+1}-\frac{\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)}{\sqrt{\beta_{t+1}+\mu}}\sqrt{H_{t+1}}\leq\max\left\{\frac{1}{2},1-\frac{\alpha\sqrt{(\beta_{t}+\mu)H_{t}}}{8\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)}\right\}H_{t}+2L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2},

We first analyze the term involving the max operator.
For any
\gamma>0
, by the AM-GM inequality, we have

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha H_{t}\sqrt{(\beta_{t}+\mu)H_{t}}}{8\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)}

\displaystyle=\frac{\alpha H_{t}\sqrt{(\beta_{t}+\mu)H_{t}}}{16\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)}+\frac{\alpha H_{t}\sqrt{\beta_{t}H_{t}}}{16\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)}+\frac{512(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(\beta_{t}+\mu)}-\frac{512(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(\beta_{t}+\mu)}

\displaystyle\geq 3\left(\frac{\alpha H_{t}\sqrt{(\beta_{t}+\mu)H_{t}}}{16\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)}\cdot\frac{\alpha H_{t}\sqrt{(\beta_{t}+\mu)H_{t}}}{16\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)}\cdot\frac{512(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(\beta_{t}+\mu)}\right)^{1/3}-\frac{512(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(\beta_{t}+\mu)}

\displaystyle=\gamma H_{t}-\frac{512(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(\beta_{t}+\mu)}.

Thus, we have

H_{t}-\frac{\alpha H_{t}\sqrt{(\beta_{t}+\mu)H_{t}}}{8\sqrt{2}\left(L_{yy}+\beta_{t}\right)}\leq(1-\gamma)H_{t}+\frac{512(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(\beta_{t}+\mu)}.

By selecting
\gamma\leq 1/2
, the right-hand side is at least
H_{t}/2
, allowing us to simplify the maximum term. Applying the AM-GM inequality (
26
) (and multiplying the last term by two to avoid irrational coefficients later), we obtain

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)H_{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\gamma)H_{t}+\frac{512(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(\beta_{t}+\mu)}+\frac{2\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)^{2}}{\gamma(\beta_{t+1}+\mu)}+2L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}.

Since
\gamma\in(0,1)
, we have
1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\in\left[\frac{1}{2},1\right)
and
\frac{1-\gamma}{1-\gamma/2}=1-\frac{\gamma}{2-\gamma}\leq 1-\frac{\gamma}{2}.
Hence, we have

\displaystyle H_{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)H_{t}+\frac{1024(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(\beta_{t}+\mu)}+\frac{4\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)^{2}}{\gamma(\beta_{t+1}+\mu)}+4L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}

By dividing both sides with
\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-(t+1)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-(t+1)}H_{t+1}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-t}H_{t}+\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-(t+1)}\left(\frac{1024(L_{yy}+\beta_{t})^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(\beta_{t}+\mu)}+\frac{4\left(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\tau_{t}+D_{{\cal Y}}(\beta_{t}-\beta_{t+1})\right)^{2}}{\gamma(\beta_{t+1}+\mu)}+4L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}\tau_{t}^{2}\right).

Using the requirements in
Condition

C.1
, and the inequalities (
28
) and (
29
),
for any
t\geq 1
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-t}H_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-(t-1)}H_{t-1}+\frac{1024(L_{yy}+C)^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(C+\mu)^{2}}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-t}\left(Ct^{b}+\mu\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{8\left((L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{(C+\mu)^{2}\gamma}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-t}\left(\frac{C}{t^{2a-b}}+\frac{\mu}{t^{2a}}\right)+\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-t}\frac{4L_{xx}D_{\cal X}^{2}}{t^{2a}}

\displaystyle\leq H_{0}+\frac{1024(L_{yy}+C)^{2}\gamma^{3}}{27\alpha^{2}(C+\mu)^{2}}\sum_{i\in[t]}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-i}\left(Ci^{b}+\mu\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{8\left((L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{(C+\mu)^{2}\gamma}\sum_{i\in[t]}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-i}\left(\frac{C}{i^{2a-b}}+\frac{\mu}{i^{2a}}\right)+\sum_{i\in[t]}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-i}\frac{4L_{xx}D_{\cal X}^{2}}{i^{2a}}.

Since
b>0
, we have

\sum_{i\in[t]}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-i}\left(Ci^{b}+\mu\right)\leq\sum_{i\in[t]}\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-i}\left(Ct^{b}+\mu\right)\leq\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-t}\frac{2\left(Ct^{b}+\mu\right)}{\gamma}.

Applying
Lemma

F.4
, for any
t
such that
t-\left\lfloor\frac{2}{\gamma}\right\rfloor\geq 0
, we have

\displaystyle H_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t}H_{0}+\frac{2048(L_{yy}+C)^{2}\gamma^{2}}{27\alpha^{2}(C+\mu)^{2}}\left(Ct^{b}+\mu\right)+\left(\frac{16\left((L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{(C+\mu)\gamma^{2}}+\frac{8L_{xx}D_{\cal X}^{2}}{\gamma}\right)\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{t-\left\lfloor\frac{2}{\gamma}\right\rfloor+1}

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{64\left((L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{(C+\mu)^{2}\gamma^{2}}\left(\frac{C(2a-b)}{t^{2a-b}}+\mu\cdot\begin{cases}\frac{E(a,0)}{t^{2a}}&0<a<1\\
\frac{\log(t)}{t^{2}}&a=1\end{cases}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\left(1-\frac{\gamma}{2}\right)^{-t}\frac{32L_{xx}D_{\cal X}^{2}}{\gamma}\cdot\begin{cases}\frac{E(a,0)}{t^{2a}}&0<a<1\\
\frac{\log(t)}{t^{2}}&a=1\end{cases}.

Using the bounds in (
30
) and setting
\gamma=t^{-a/2}/2
yield

\displaystyle H_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\left(H_{0}+\frac{64\left((L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)e}{C}t^{a}+16eL_{xx}D_{\cal X}^{2}t^{a/2}+64L_{xx}D_{\cal X}^{2}\cdot\begin{cases}\frac{E(a,0)}{t^{3a/2}}&0<a<1\\
\frac{\log(t)}{t^{3/2}}&a=1\end{cases}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{t^{1-a/2}}{4}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{512(L_{yy}+C)^{2}}{27\alpha^{2}Ct^{a-b}}+\frac{256E(a,b)\left((L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}+b^{2}(CD_{{\cal Y}})^{2}\right)}{Ct^{a-b}}.

If
Assumption

1.1
additionally holds with
\mu>0
and
C=0
, then

\displaystyle H_{t}

\displaystyle\leq\left(H_{0}+\frac{64(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}e}{\mu}t^{a}+16eL_{xx}D_{\cal X}^{2}t^{a/2}+64L_{xx}D_{\cal X}^{2}\cdot\begin{cases}\frac{E(a,0)}{t^{3a/2}}&0<a<1\\
\frac{\log(t)}{t^{3/2}}&a=1\end{cases}\right)\exp\left(-\frac{t^{1-a/2}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\frac{512L_{yy}}{27\alpha^{2}\mu t^{a}}+\frac{256(L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}})^{2}}{\mu}\begin{cases}\frac{E(a,0)}{t^{a}}&0<a<1\\
\frac{\log(t)}{t}&a=1\end{cases}.

Recall that applying
Lemma

F.4
requires
t-\left\lfloor\frac{2}{\gamma}\right\rfloor\geq 0
. With our choices of
\gamma
, this condition reduces to
t-\left\lfloor 4t^{1/2}\right\rfloor\geq 0
, which holds for all
t\geq 14
. Therefore, the existence of such
M_{3}
and
M_{4}
is guaranteed. This competes the proof.
∎

With these refined bounds on the discrepancy sequence
\{H_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
established, we are now ready to derive the convergence guarantees for
Algorithm

1
across the nonconvex and convex settings discussed in
Section

2
.

C.1
Proof of
Theorem

2.1

The proof relies on the following lemma, which establishes an upper bound on the accumulated LMO gap provided that the discrepancy sequence
\{H_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
exhibits a non-asymptotic decay rate.

Lemma C.4
.

Suppose
Assumption

1.1
holds. Let
\{x_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
be a primal LMO sequence with stepsize
\tau_{t}=(t+1)^{-a}
generated by the update rule (
15a
), let
\{y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}\subset{\cal Y}
be any dual sequence, and let
\beta_{t}=C(t+1)^{-b}
.
Suppose
2a>b
,
a,b\in(0,1]
,
C\geq 0
,
\max\{C,\mu\}>0
and we have

H_{t}\leq\frac{M_{t}}{(t+1)^{c}},\quad\forall t\geq 0

for some non-decreasing, positive sequence
\{M_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
and a constant
c
satisfying
0<c\leq 2a-b
if
C>0
and
0<c\leq 2a
if
C=0
and
\mu>0
.
Then, for any
T\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\sum_{t=0}^{T}G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x_{t},y_{t})

\displaystyle\leq\left(2\max_{x\in{\cal X}}\left|f(x)\right|+CD_{{\cal Y}}^{2}\right)(T+1)^{a}+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{2L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{2CM_{t}}+CL_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+CL_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}/(C+\mu)}{2(C+\mu)(t+1)^{(c-b)/2}}

\displaystyle\quad+\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{2L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{2\mu M_{t}}+\mu L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{\mu L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{C+\mu}}{2(C+\mu)(t+1)^{c/2}}.

Proof.

We bound the the last inequality in
Lemma

A.6
. Observe that

\displaystyle L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}+\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{\frac{2M_{t}}{C(t+1)^{c-b}+\mu(t+1)^{2a+c}}}+\frac{L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2(t+1)^{a}}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2C(t+1)^{a-b}+2\mu(t+1)^{a}}.

Using Cauchy-Schwartz inequality, we have

\displaystyle\left(\frac{C}{(t+1)^{c-b}}+\frac{\mu}{(t+1)^{c}}\right)\left(C(t+1)^{c-b}+\mu(t+1)^{c}\right)\geq(C+\mu)^{2}

\displaystyle\implies

\displaystyle\frac{1}{C(t+1)^{c-b}+\mu(t+1)^{c}}\leq\frac{1}{(C+\mu)^{2}}\left(\frac{C}{(t+1)^{c-b}}+\frac{\mu}{(t+1)^{c}}\right),

and similarly,

\frac{1}{C(t+1)^{a-b}+\mu(t+1)^{a}}\leq\frac{1}{(C+\mu)^{2}}\left(\frac{C}{(t+1)^{a-b}}+\frac{\mu}{(t+1)^{a}}\right).

Thus, we can further bound the above inequality as follows

\displaystyle L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}+\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{\frac{2M_{t}}{(C+\mu)^{2}}\left(\frac{C}{(t+1)^{c-b}}+\frac{\mu}{(t+1)^{c}}\right)}+\frac{L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2(t+1)^{a}}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2(C+\mu)^{2}}\left(\frac{C}{(t+1)^{a-b}}+\frac{\mu}{(t+1)^{a}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{2CM_{t}}}{(C+\mu)(t+1)^{(c-b)/2}}+\frac{L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{2\mu M_{t}}}{(C+\mu)(t+1)^{c/2}}+\frac{L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2(t+1)^{a}}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2(C+\mu)^{2}}\left(\frac{C}{(t+1)^{a-b}}+\frac{\mu}{(t+1)^{a}}\right),

where the last inequality follows from the basic inequality
\sqrt{x+y}\leq\sqrt{x}+\sqrt{y}
. Using the requirement
0<c\leq 2a-b
, we may conclude that

\displaystyle L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{\frac{2H_{t}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}}+\left(L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+\frac{L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{\beta_{t}+\mu}\right)\frac{\tau_{t}}{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{2CM_{t}}+\frac{CL_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{C+\mu}}{(C+\mu)(t+1)^{(c-b)/2}}+\frac{L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{2\mu M_{t}}+\frac{\mu L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{C+\mu}}{(C+\mu)(t+1)^{c/2}}+\frac{L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2(t+1)^{a}}

The claim follows by a simple rearrangement of the inequality above and applying
Lemma

A.6
.
∎

We now provide the detailed versions of the results summarized in
Theorem

2.1
, including explicit constants and optimal parameter configurations. We first address the settings without additional geometric assumptions on
{\cal Y}
.

Theorem C.5

(Detailed version of
Theorem

2.1

(i)
–
(ii)
)
.

Suppose
\{x_{t},y_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
is the sequence generated by
Algorithm

1
under
\{\tau_{t},\beta_{t}\}_{t\geq 0}
satisfying
Condition

C.1
with
2a>b,a,b\in(0,1)
. If
Assumption

1.1
holds, then for any
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{T}G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x_{t},y_{t})}{T+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\max_{x\in{\cal X}}\left|f(x)\right|+CD_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{(T+1)^{1-a}}+\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{2L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{2CM_{1}}+CL_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2C(t+1)^{(a-2b)/3}},

\displaystyle G_{\cal Y}(x_{T},y_{T})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{1}}{(T+1)^{(2a-b)/3}}+\frac{CD_{{\cal Y}}^{2}}{2(T+1)^{b}}.

a,b
, we obtain the optimal rate of
O(T^{-1/6})
when

. If
Assumption

1.1
holds with
\mu>0
and
C=0
, then for any
T\geq 0
,

\displaystyle\frac{\sum_{t=0}^{T}G_{\cal X}^{\LMO}(x_{t},y_{t})}{T+1}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\max_{x\in{\cal X}}\left|f(x)\right|}{(T+1)^{1-a}}+\frac{1}{T+1}\sum_{t=0}^{T}\frac{2L_{yx}D_{{\cal X}}\sqrt{2\mu M_{2}}+\mu L_{xx}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}+L_{yx}^{2}D_{{\cal X}}^{2}}{2\mu(t+1)^{a/3}},

\displaystyle G_{\cal Y}(x_{T},y_{T})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M_{2}}{(T+1)^{2a/3}},

By optimizing over
a
, we obtain the optimal rate of
O(T^{-1/4})
when

.

C.4
. Specifically, by

, the decay condition on

required in

is satisfied with constant

, where we set
b=0
when
\mu>0
. Moreover, the conditions on
c
in

C.4
are trivially satisfied since
(2a-b)/3\leq 2a-b
and
2a/3\leq 2a
.
On the other hand, the bounds on
G_{\cal Y}
follow from applying

A.6
together with

C.2
. This completes the proof.
